## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
The target is the BLUE-walled strip (BLACK floor). Robots need to reach and stay in the BLACK floor region. The score increases for every robot outside the target. So robots should navigate to the BLACK floor area and stay there.

**Key perceptions:**
- Floor color BLACK = target region (blue walls)
- Floor color GRAY = middle region
- Floor color WHITE = not applicable here
- Color 3 = blue (walls of target region)

**Champion Analysis (score 12622):**
- State 0: Spiral, transitions on seeing color 3 (blue walls) or few neighbors → go to state 1
- State 1: Repulsion, transitions on few neighbors/gray floor/black floor → other states
- State 2: Exploration, transitions on gray floor → back to state 0

Wait, let me re-read. The target is the BLACK floor strip with BLUE walls. So robots should:
1. Navigate toward blue walls (GoToColor with clr=3)
2. Stay on black floor

**Better strategy:**
- State A: GoToColor blue (clr=3) to find the blue-walled strip
- State B: Stop or exploration once on black floor
- Transition: if floor is BLACK → stop/stay

**Plan:**
1. Elitism: Copy champion exactly
2. Exploitation: Small edits of champion
3. Exploration: New topologies focused on GoToColor blue + stay on black floor

FSM designs:
- Simple 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → if black floor, stop
- 3-state with GoToColor + black floor detection
- Variations of champion with tweaked params

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion (exact copy)**
2. **Exploit champion**: tweak rep gain, probabilities
3. **Exploit champion**: tweak spiral speed, transition probs
4. **Exploit champion**: add transition from state2 on black floor
5. **New**: GoToColor(blue) + Stop when black
6. **New**: GoToColor(blue) + exploration when gray
7. **New**: 3-state with GoToColor, stop on black, explore
8. **New**: GoAwayColor(green=2) to avoid green walls
9. **New**: Exploration with black floor detection to stop
10. **New**: 2-state GoToColor blue + random walk
11. **Exploit best repulsion simple**
12. **Exploit**: GoToColor blue alone
13. **New 3-state**: GoToColor → stay on black → explore if gray
14. **Mixed**: attraction + GoToColor

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 2549):**
The champion is a 2-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue, clr=3) - moves toward blue walls
- Transition: if floor is BLACK (c=0, p=0.95) → go to state 1
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=30) - random walk
- Transitions: if GRAY (c=1, p=0.5) → go back to state 0; if BLACK (c=0, p=0.8) → stay in state 0

Wait, let me re-read: state 1 transitions go to destination 0 (which is the OTHER state, i.e., state 0). So when on gray floor, go back to GoToColor. When on black floor, also go back to GoToColor? That seems odd.

Actually looking at this: both transitions from state 1 go to destination 0 (the other state = state 0). So state 1 (exploration) transitions to state 0 (GoToColor) when gray or black. This means exploration is brief - quickly returns to GoToColor.

The key insight: **GoToColor(blue) gets robots to blue-walled strip, black floor detection confirms arrival, then brief exploration keeps them moving within the strip.**

**Strategy for improvement:**
- The champion works well. Try variations with:
  - Higher black floor probability (p=0.99)
  - Different rwm values
  - Adding a Stop state when on black floor
  - 3-state with GoToColor → Stop on black → GoToColor if gray
  - Tweaking transition probabilities

**Plan:**
1. Exact champion copy
2. Exploit: increase black detection prob to 0.99
3. Exploit: lower rwm for tighter exploration
4. Exploit: add Stop state on black floor
5. Exploit: 3-state with Stop instead of exploration
6. Exploit: higher p for gray transition back
7. Exploit: combine with GoAwayColor(green=2)
8. New: GoToColor → Stop directly on black
9. Exploit best #2 with tweaks
10. New: 3-state GoToColor + Stop + GoToColor
11. Exploit: different exploration rate
12. New: 4-state variation
13. Exploit #3 with tweaks
14. Exploit champion with extra transition

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insight from rounds 2-3:**
The champion and all top performers share the same 2-state topology:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue, clr=3) → transition to state 1 when on BLACK floor (high probability ~0.95-0.99)
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=15-50) → transition back to state 0 when on GRAY (p~0.5-0.6) or BLACK (p~0.7-0.9)

The scores cluster around 2549-2709 for the best variants. The differences are:
- rwm: 15, 30, 50 all work similarly
- p for black detection in state 0: 0.95-0.99
- p for gray/black transitions back to GoToColor

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Fine-tune the champion's parameters more carefully
3. Try rwm values between 15-30 (e.g., 20, 25)
4. Try slightly different black floor probabilities (0.97, 0.98)
5. Try adding a small probability of going back to GoToColor when seeing blue (c=7, l=3) in state 1
6. Try Stop instead of exploration for state 1 (robots just stay when on black)
7. Explore whether 3-state machines can beat 2-state

The key observation: state 1 (exploration) with transitions back to GoToColor when gray/black means robots quickly return to GoToColor if they drift off black floor. This creates a cycle: find blue wall → reach black floor → briefly explore → if drift to gray, go back to GoToColor.

Let me try:
- rwm=20, 25 (between best 15 and 30)
- p_black=0.97 (between 0.95 and 0.99)
- Combining best p_gray=0.6 with best p_black=0.9
- Stop state variant
- Adding blue-perception trigger to state 1

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 2518):**
The new champion is a 3-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if BLACK floor (p=0.95) → state 1; if GRAY (p=0.03) → state 1
- State 1: Exploration (rwm=20) → if GRAY (p=0.5) → state 1 (self?); wait, destination is indexed among OTHER states
  - With 3 states, leaving state 1, others are [0, 2]: D=0→state 0, D=1→state 2
  - So: n1x0: D=1→state 2 (GoToColor2); n1x1: D=0→state 0 (GoToColor1)
- State 2: GoToColor(blue) → if BLACK (p=0.9) → state 1 (exploration)

So it's: GoToColor → (on black) → Explore → (on gray→GoToColor2 or on black→GoToColor1) → GoToColor → (on black) → Explore...

Actually both GoToColor states do the same thing. The key is: two GoToColor states and one exploration state, creating a robust loop.

**Key observations:**
- 3-state with two GoToColor(blue) instances works slightly better than 2-state
- rwm=15-20 seems better than 30-50
- p_black=0.95-0.97 for detection
- Low probability of gray transition from GoToColor (0.03) keeps robots searching
- Best performer #2 adds blue-wall perception trigger in exploration state

**Strategy:**
1. Exact champion copy
2. Exploit champion: tweak rwm to 15, 25
3. Exploit champion: adjust p_black to 0.97, 0.99
4. Exploit champion: add extra transitions
5. Exploit #2: tweak its parameters
6. Explore: 4-state variants
7. Try Stop state in black region

Let me focus on fine-tuning the 3-state topology that's working best.

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score 2131):**
The 4-state champion:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → BLACK(p=0.95)→state1; GRAY(p=0.03)→state1
- State 1: Exploration(rwm=20) → GRAY(p=0.5)→state2(GoToColor); BLACK(p=0.85)→state0(GoToColor); blue-visible(p=0.5)→state2(GoToColor)
- State 2: GoToColor(blue) → BLACK(p=0.9)→state1(Explore)
- State 3: Stop → GRAY(p=0.6)→state0(GoToColor); random(p=0.02)→state2(GoToColor)

State 3 (Stop) is reachable only from... wait, nothing points to state 3! Let me re-read.

With 4 states, destinations are indexed among OTHER states:
- From state 0 (others=[1,2,3]): D=0→s1, D=1→s2, D=2→s3... but n0x0 D=1 means state 2, n0x1 D=1 means state 2
- Actually D indexes into the list of OTHER states. From state 0, others are [1,2,3], so D=0→s1, D=1→s2, D=2→s3

So state 0→state1 and state0→state1 (both D=1 meaning s2? No...)

Let me re-read: n0x0 D=1 → from state 0, others=[1,2,3], D=1 means state 2. n0x1 D=1 → also state 2.
State 1: n1x0 D=2 → from state 1, others=[0,2,3], D=2 means state 3. n1x1 D=0→state0. n1x2 D=2→state3.
State 2: n2x0 D=1 → from state 2, others=[0,1,3], D=1 means state 1.
State 3: n3x0 D=0→state0. n3x1 D=2→state2.

So: State0(GoToColor)→state2(GoToColor) on black/gray. State1(Explore)→state3(Stop) on gray/blue; →state0(GoToColor) on black. State2(GoToColor)→state1(Explore) on black. State3(Stop)→state0(GoToColor) on gray; →state2(GoToColor) on random.

The Stop state acts as a "holding" state. Robots stop when they arrive, then resume GoToColor.

**Key insight:** The 4-state topology with Stop state is working better. The Stop state keeps robots in place on black floor.

**Strategy for round 6:**
1. Exact champion copy
2. Exploit champion: tweak probabilities (p_black, p_gray transitions)
3. Try making state 3 more accessible (point more transitions to it)
4. Try different rwm values for exploration
5. Try adding blue-perception trigger in state 2
6. Exploit best #2 (3-state, score 2153)
7. Explore 4-state with different configurations

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 14 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.49 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
